Browse All / Marfan Syndrome

Marfan Syndrome

Genetic MONDO:0007947 Pathograph 22 Show in embeddings browser Connective Tissue Disorder

Marfan syndrome is an autosomal dominant connective tissue disorder caused by mutations in FBN1, which encodes fibrillin-1. It affects multiple organ systems including the cardiovascular system (aortic root dilation and dissection), skeleton (tall stature, arachnodactyly, scoliosis), and eyes (ectopia lentis).

Definitions

Orphanet disease definition
Orphanet defines Marfan syndrome as a systemic connective-tissue disease with cardiovascular, musculo-skeletal, ophthalmic, and pulmonary manifestations.

Mechanistic Hypotheses

1
Canonical FBN1 Loss / TGF-β Dysregulation Model
canonical_fbn1_tgfb_dysregulation_model CANONICAL
Evidence balance 1 support
Heterozygous FBN1 pathogenic variants reduce or qualitatively alter fibrillin-1 microfibrils, the extracellular scaffold that both confers elastic-fiber mechanical integrity and sequesters latent TGF-β complexes. Loss of microfibrillar function therefore produces a dual lesion: (1) structural weakness of elastin-rich tissues (aortic media, suspensory ligament of the lens, dura, lung, skin, mitral valve) and (2) excess bioavailable TGF-β with downstream Smad2/3 and non-canonical pathway activation in aortic wall and other tissues. The result is the characteristic multisystem phenotype — progressive aortic root aneurysm and dissection, ectopia lentis, dolichostenomelia, scoliosis, dural ectasia, mitral valve prolapse, and pulmonary blebs. Losartan and other angiotensin/TGF-β-axis inhibitors that reduce aortic-wall TGF-β signaling provide interventional support for the TGF-β-dysregulation component.
Retained as CANONICAL. The 2026 falcon hypothesis-search report (kb/hypotheses/Marfan_Syndrome/canonical_fbn1_tgfb_dysregulation_model; openscientist timed out) confirms the dual-lesion model: heterozygous FBN1 variants cause microfibrillar mechanical failure AND excess bioavailable TGF-β with downstream Smad2/3 activation. Three qualifications: (1) the relative contribution of structural-weakness vs TGF-β-signaling components varies by tissue and variant — ectopia lentis is primarily structural while aortic aneurysm involves substantial TGF-β-driven medial degeneration and matrix remodeling; (2) losartan's clinical benefit on aortic root growth in pediatric MFS is modest in head-to-head trials with atenolol, indicating that TGF-β antagonism alone does not fully prevent dissection risk and that hemodynamic stress reduction remains critical; (3) Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1/TGFBR2 mutations) and related TGF-β-pathway disorders display a more aggressive aneurysm phenotype, indicating that the TGF-β-axis can be pathogenic even when fibrillin-1 is intact, qualifying the necessity of microfibrillar disruption for the aortic phenotype.
Deep research

Pathophysiology

6
FBN1 Gene Mutation
Mutations in the FBN1 gene result in defective fibrillin-1 protein, which affects the elasticity and strength of connective tissues.

Dysregulated TGF-beta Signaling Defective fibrillin microfibrils fail to properly sequester latent TGF-beta complexes.
Extracellular Matrix Remodeling Loss of normal fibrillin scaffold destabilizes elastic fiber architecture and ECM integrity.
Impaired Mechanotransduction Altered fibrillin-integrin interactions impair mechanosensing in load-bearing connective tissues.

PMID:8180508 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The gene encoding for fibrillin, the FBN1 gene on chromosome 15, has been identified as the defective gene causing the Marfan syndrome. Fibrillin is the large glycoprotein with a repetitive domain structure and is a major protein component of microfibrils, a fibrillar system closely associated..."
The statement is supported by the identification of the FBN1 gene as the cause of Marfan syndrome and its role in the structure of connective tissue.
PMID:39096853 SUPPORT In vitro / ex vivo
"Marfan syndrome (MFS) is a hereditary condition caused by mutations in the FBN1 gene. Genetic mutations in the FBN1 locus impact the function of the encoded protein, Fibrillin 1, a structural molecule forming microfibrils found in the connective tissue."
The statement is supported by the description of FBN1 mutations affecting the function of fibrillin-1, which is crucial for connective tissue structure.
PMID:17657824 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Mutations in the FBN1 gene have been characterised in patients affected by Marfan syndrome and Marfan-related disorders."
The statement is supported by the identification of FBN1 mutations in Marfan syndrome patients.
+ 5 more references
Dysregulated TGF-beta Signaling
Defective fibrillin-1 microfibrils fail to sequester latent TGF-beta complexes, leading to increased bioavailable TGF-beta and excessive SMAD2/3 signaling that drives vascular smooth muscle cell phenotypic switching and ECM remodeling. The weight of this step is contested and stage-dependent: in young Fbn1 C1039G/+ mice aortopathy develops without detectable change in smooth muscle TGF-beta signalling, and removing that signalling makes it worse.

Downstream
Extracellular Matrix Remodeling Excess SMAD signaling increases protease activity and maladaptive ECM turnover.
Aortic Root Aneurysm Persistent TGF-beta activity accelerates medial degeneration and progressive root dilation.
Tall Stature Dysregulated growth factor signaling contributes to long bone overgrowth.
Arachnodactyly Abnormal connective tissue growth signaling contributes to elongated digits.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:12598898 SUPPORT Model organism
"We show that mice deficient in fibrillin-1 have marked dysregulation of transforming growth factor-beta (TGF-beta) activation and signaling, resulting in apoptosis in the developing lung."
Fibrillin-1 deficiency dysregulates TGF-beta activation and signalling in a Marfan mouse model, shown here in the developing lung.
PMID:16601194 SUPPORT Model organism
"We show that aortic aneurysm in a mouse model of MFS is associated with increased TGF-beta signaling and can be prevented by TGF-beta antagonists such as TGF-beta-neutralizing antibody or the angiotensin II type 1 receptor (AT1) blocker, losartan."
Links increased TGF-beta signalling to aortic aneurysm in a Marfan mouse model, and shows that antagonising it prevents the aneurysm.
PMID:28119285 REFUTE Model organism
"aortopathy develops in the absence of detectable alterations in SMC TGF-β signaling."
Contrary evidence: in young Fbn1 C1039G/+ mice the aortopathy appears without any detectable change in smooth muscle TGF-beta signalling, so increased signalling is not required for it to start.
+ 1 more reference
Extracellular Matrix Remodeling
Increased matrix metalloproteinase activity, elastic fiber fragmentation, altered collagen composition, and proteoglycan accumulation (especially versican) weaken the aortic wall medial layer.

Downstream
Aortic Aneurysm Elastic fiber fragmentation and collagen disarray weaken the aortic wall.
Mitral Valve Prolapse Connective tissue degeneration in valve leaflets promotes prolapse.
Ectopia Lentis Weak zonular microfibrils predispose to lens displacement.
Scoliosis Ligamentous laxity and vertebral connective tissue weakness promote spinal curvature.
Spontaneous Pneumothorax Fragile connective tissue in lung apices increases risk of spontaneous pneumothorax.
Pulmonary Artery Dilatation Fibrillin-1 deficiency and medial weakness affect the great vessels beyond the aorta, dilating the main pulmonary artery.
Retinal Detachment Weak ocular microfibrils, axial globe elongation, and zonular instability predispose to retinal detachment.
Dural Ectasia Weakened dural connective tissue stretches under chronic cerebrospinal-fluid pulsation, producing lumbosacral dural ectasia.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:15254584 SUPPORT Model organism
"Informatively, use of homologous recombination to generate mice heterozygous for a comparable missense mutation (C1039G) revealed impaired microfibrillar deposition"
A heterozygous Fbn1 missense mutation impairs microfibril deposition in the matrix, the defect the medial matrix changes start from.
PMID:10097121 SUPPORT Model organism
"Histopathological analysis of mgR/mgR specimens implicates medial calcification, the inflammatory-fibroproliferative response, and inflammation-mediated elastolysis in the natural history of dissecting aneurysm."
In mice underexpressing fibrillin-1, the aortic media degenerates through elastolysis and fibroproliferation on the way to dissecting aneurysm.
Impaired Mechanotransduction
Perturbed integrin-fibrillin interactions alter focal adhesion signaling and mechanosensing, contributing to maladaptive vascular smooth muscle cell responses to hemodynamic stress. Nitric oxide signalling through soluble guanylate cyclase and PRKG1 also dysregulates the smooth muscle actin cytoskeleton. NO donors produce Marfan-like aortopathy in wild-type mice, and inhibiting either enzyme reverses the aortopathy in Marfan mice.

Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33976159 SUPPORT Model organism
"Here, we show that nitric oxide (NO) signaling dysregulates actin cytoskeleton dynamics in Marfan Syndrome smooth muscle cells and that NO-donors induce Marfan-like aortopathy in wild-type mice, indicating that a marked increase in NO suffices to induce aortopathy."
Shows the NO route to cytoskeletal dysfunction in Marfan smooth muscle cells, and that raising NO alone is enough to produce the aortopathy.
PMID:33976159 SUPPORT Model organism
"Marfan aortopathy in mice is reverted by pharmacological inhibition of soluble guanylate cyclase and cGMP-dependent protein kinase and lentiviral-mediated Prkg1 silencing."
The reversal by blocking sGC or PRKG1 makes this pathway a cause of the aortopathy rather than a marker of it.
Vascular Inflammation
Angiotensin II type 1 receptor signaling and chemokine pathways recruit macrophages and promote inflammatory mediators that exacerbate vascular smooth muscle cell dysfunction and ECM degradation. Aneurysmal aortas from Marfan patients carry increased T lymphocytes and macrophages in the media and adventitia, as do familial and sporadic thoracic aortic aneurysms.

Aortic Dissection Inflammatory ECM damage and smooth muscle dysfunction increase risk of acute intimal tearing.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18954631 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Immunohistochemistry of thoracic aortic aneurysm aortas demonstrated that the media and adventitia from Marfan syndrome, familial thoracic aortic aneurysm, and sporadic cases had increased numbers of T lymphocytes and macrophages when compared with control aortas."
Direct human evidence of the inflammatory infiltrate in Marfan aneurysm tissue (five Marfan aortas against five controls), against the older view of medial degeneration as non-inflammatory.
Mitochondrial Dysfunction
Emerging evidence implicates mitochondrial dysfunction and oxidative stress in vascular smooth muscle cells and endothelial cells as contributors to thoracic aortic aneurysm progression in Marfan syndrome. Transcriptomic and metabolic profiling of Marfan mouse and patient aortas shows mitochondrial dysfunction with mitochondrial DNA depletion, and depleting the mitochondrial transcription factor Tfam in vascular smooth muscle cells is enough to cause aortic aneurysm.

Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:33709773 SUPPORT Model organism
"Combining transcriptomics and metabolic analysis of aortas from an MFS mouse model (Fbn1c1039g/+) and MFS patients, we have identified mitochondrial dysfunction alongside with mtDNA depletion as a new hallmark of aortic aneurysm disease in MFS."
Identifies mitochondrial dysfunction and mtDNA depletion in Marfan mouse and patient aortas.
PMID:33709773 SUPPORT Model organism
"Conditional Tfam-deficient VSMC mice lose their contractile capacity, showed aortic aneurysms, and died prematurely."
The conditional Tfam model tests the mitochondrial step directly in smooth muscle cells, which is the basis for treating it as a cause rather than a correlate.
PMID:33709773 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Similar results were found in MFS patients."
The increased lactate production and decreased oxygen consumption seen in Fbn1-silenced smooth muscle cells were also found in Marfan patients, giving the mitochondrial node human support alongside the mouse data.

Medical Actions

5
Beta Blockers
Action: beta adrenergic agent therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is beta adrenergic agent therapy, annotated with Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Pharmacotherapy NCIT:C15986
Used to reduce stress on the aorta and prevent its enlargement.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36939732 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Beta-blockers remain the mainstay of therapy as they have proven to slow aortic enlargement. Angiotensin receptor blockers are a useful alternative and with proven benefit as an add-on therapy to limit aortic growth."
Beta-blockers are used to slow aortic enlargement, which indirectly reduces stress on the aorta. However, they do not prevent aortic enlargement entirely.
PMID:17845137 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Traditional medical therapies, such as beta-adrenergic receptor blockade, are used to slow pathologic aortic growth and decrease the risk of aortic dissection by decreasing hemodynamic stress."
Beta-blockers are used to slow aortic growth and decrease hemodynamic stress, but they do not completely prevent aortic enlargement.
PMID:17376944 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Medical treatment with beta-blockers is probably helpful in most children with aortic root dilatation."
Beta-blockers are helpful in slowing the progression of aortic root dilatation, but they do not completely prevent it.
Angiotensin Receptor Blockers
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: losartan CHEBI:6541 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses losartan (CHEBI:6541). CHEBI:6541 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
ARBs such as losartan reduce aortic root growth by blocking angiotensin II type 1 receptor signaling and modulating TGF-beta pathways. Pediatric data show superior tolerability and efficacy compared to beta-blockers.

Environmental Factors

2
Vigorous Isometric and Competitive Sport Restriction
Vigorous isometric and competitive sport exposure ECTO:6000031 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is Vigorous isometric and competitive sport exposure, annotated with exposure to strenuous exercise (ECTO:6000031). ECTO:6000031 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Deliberately scoped to the restriction half of activity modification. The complementary, aortoprotective effect of moderate dynamic (aerobic) activity is curated as the separate "Moderate Dynamic Aerobic Activity" exposure below, so that exercise-benefit evidence is no longer attached to a restriction claim (issue dismech#6953). Both exposures are held simultaneously in current practice: exercise intensity and type, not physical activity per se, are what is restricted. This node and the moderate-activity node below previously shared XCO:0000059, which made the KGX exporter emit two identical subject/predicate/ object edges; they now carry distinct ECTO terms (ECTO:6000031 strenuous vs ECTO:6000002 exercise), which the schema also prefers over XCO, so the split is visible downstream. OPEN QUESTION: the exported subject is the exposure (strenuous exercise) while this node's effect is phrased for the protective restriction, so the edge still reads "strenuous exercise reduces risk". Whether to keep both arms PROTECTIVE or re-express this one as "increases risk" is left to the structured effect_polarity enum in issue dismech#2656.
Avoidance of maximal-effort isometric/static exertion (heavy weight lifting), high-intensity exercise, and competitive or contact sport, which acutely raise blood pressure and hemodynamic stress on a fragile aortic wall.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:9789865 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The person with Marfan syndrome is often tall and agile and may unknowingly participate in certain physical activities and sports, putting himself or herself at risk for aortic dissection and sudden death."
The article suggests that physical activity restrictions are necessary to prevent aortic dissection in individuals with Marfan syndrome.
PMID:36072558 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Current guidelines for patients with thoracic aortic aneurysms or dissections (TAD) restrict vigorous exertion with the intention to prevent acute aortic dissections."
Documents that the restriction applies specifically to vigorous exertion and that its stated intent is prevention of acute aortic dissection.
PMID:35144638 SUPPORT INDIRECT Model organism
"There were potential beneficial effects of PA on arterial structures, but this review also showed deleterious effects when PA was conducted at high intensities, corresponding to 75-85% of the maximal oxygen uptake. However, these effects have only been reported in animal studies."
Systematic review supporting an intensity threshold for harm rather than a blanket restriction on activity. INDIRECT because the review states explicitly that the deleterious high-intensity effects have been reported only in animal studies, so it does not establish the harm arm in humans. Tagged MODEL_ORGANISM because the specific harm claim this item is cited for is animal-derived by the authors' own statement, even though the review as a whole also surveys human data.
Mechanism Target:
PROTECTS_AGAINST Aortic Dissection — Restriction of vigorous isometric and competitive exertion is intended to prevent aortic dissection by limiting acute rises in blood pressure and aortic wall stress.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36072558 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Current guidelines for patients with thoracic aortic aneurysms or dissections (TAD) restrict vigorous exertion with the intention to prevent acute aortic dissections."
States that current guidelines restrict vigorous exertion with the intention of preventing acute aortic events. An intention, which is what guidelines encode, not a demonstrated effect. The exposure binding denotes strenuous exercise rather than its restriction, creating a polarity problem for the protective relationship (issue 2656). The intention to prevent dissection is not a measured reduction in risk.
PMID:9789865 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The person with Marfan syndrome is often tall and agile and may unknowingly participate in certain physical activities and sports, putting himself or herself at risk for aortic dissection and sudden death."
Notes that affected people are often tall and agile and may unknowingly take part in activities that put them at risk of dissection and sudden death. It establishes the hazard the restriction addresses.
Moderate Dynamic Aerobic Activity
Moderate dynamic aerobic activity exposure ECTO:6000002 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is Moderate dynamic aerobic activity exposure, annotated with exposure to exercise (ECTO:6000002). ECTO:6000002 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Split out from the former single "Physical Activity Restrictions" node so that exercise-benefit evidence attaches to a claim it actually supports (issue dismech#6953). The human trial evidence is pediatric and single-centre and the supporting aortic-wall data are murine, so the aortoprotective claim should be read as emerging rather than settled.
Regular moderate-intensity dynamic (aerobic) activity undertaken within the restrictions above - for example a 10,000-steps-per-day walking target - rather than sedentary avoidance of all exertion.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36453629 SUPPORT Human clinical
"We instructed patients to take 10 000 steps per day, tracked by an activity tracker... AoR Z score in the intervention group had a significantly lower rate of change per year compared with the controls (rate of change, -0.24 versus +0.008; P=0.01)."
A prescribed moderate dynamic activity target slowed the rate of aortic root growth relative to controls, directly supporting an aortoprotective effect of moderate aerobic activity.
PMID:36072558 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Moderate intensity exercise is safe and feasible for many TAD patients."
Establishes that moderate-intensity exercise is safe and feasible in thoracic aortic disease - a necessary but not sufficient part of this node's claim. PARTIAL rather than SUPPORT because safety is not aortoprotection: the paper does not report that moderate activity slows aortic root dilation. Note also that the cohort is mixed heritable-plus-degenerative TAD (31 cases, only 2 with Marfan syndrome), so it generalizes to Marfan rather than measuring it directly. The aortoprotective claim itself rests on PMID:36453629 and PMID:28947563.
PMID:28947563 SUPPORT Model organism
"Over the 5-month experimental period, aortic root dilation rate was significantly greater in the sedentary MF group, compared with the wild-type group (∆mm, 0.27±0.07 versus 0.13±0.02, respectively). Exercise significantly blunted the aortic root dilation rate in MF mice compared with sedentary..."
Moderate exercise training blunted aortic root dilation in a Marfan mouse model, giving the aortoprotective direction of effect a mechanistic counterpart in vivo.
Mechanism Target:
PROTECTS_AGAINST Aortic Root Aneurysm — The complementary half of activity modification, and the better-evidenced of the two protective links: this one has an intervention. Patients instructed to walk ten thousand steps a day had a significantly lower aortic root Z score than controls, which measures the outcome this node holds rather than an intention. Held simultaneously with the restriction link by design, since what is restricted is exercise intensity and type rather than physical activity itself.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36453629 SUPPORT Human clinical
"We instructed patients to take 10 000 steps per day, tracked by an activity tracker... AoR Z score in the intervention group had a significantly lower rate of change per year compared with the controls (rate of change, -0.24 versus +0.008; P=0.01)."
Intervention study in which patients instructed to walk ten thousand steps a day had a significantly lower aortic root Z score. A measured effect on the dilation this node describes.
PMID:28947563 SUPPORT Model organism
"Over the 5-month experimental period, aortic root dilation rate was significantly greater in the sedentary MF group, compared with the wild-type group (∆mm, 0.27±0.07 versus 0.13±0.02, respectively). Exercise significantly blunted the aortic root dilation rate in MF mice compared with sedentary..."
Aortic root dilation was significantly greater in sedentary mutant animals than in wild type over five months. Converges from an animal model, so it is carried at partial and not alone.

Biochemical Markers

1
Fibrillin-1 Protein (Abnormal)
Context: Can be used in research settings but not routine diagnostics.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:8180508 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Analysis of fibrillin protein or gene defects in individuals with related phenotypes has revealed that a perinatal lethal syndrome, termed neonatal Marfan syndrome, is due to FBN1 gene mutations."
Direct analysis of fibrillin protein and gene defects in affected individuals supports an abnormal fibrillin-1 protein state across the Marfan spectrum.
PMID:22705998 SUPPORT Model organism
"Although historically Marfan syndrome (MFS) has always been considered as a condition caused by the deficiency of a structural extracellular matrix protein, fibrillin-1, the study of Marfan mouse models and Marfan-related conditions has shifted our current understanding to a pathogenic model..."
Affirms fibrillin-1 deficiency as the underlying protein abnormality, but only partially supports a protein-centric reading of the disease because the review argues the pathogenic model is better framed as TGF-beta dysregulation downstream of that deficiency.
PMID:37688493 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Marfan syndrome (MFS) is a multisystem genetic disorder with over 3000 mutations described in the fibrillin 1 (FBN1) gene."
The very large described FBN1 mutation spectrum supports an abnormal fibrillin-1 protein product as the expected biochemical state in Marfan syndrome.

Diagnosis

4
Revised Ghent Nosology
The diagnosis rests on the 2010 revised Ghent nosology, which gives most weight to the cardiovascular manifestations. Aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis are its cardinal clinical features. Without a family history, the two together are sufficient for the diagnosis; when either is absent, a bona fide FBN1 mutation or a systemic score of the other features is required.
Clinical Evaluation NCIT:C124351 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Results: In the absence of family history, aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis together establish the diagnosis. If either is absent, a bona fide FBN1 mutation or a sufficient systemic score is also required.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20591885 SUPPORT Other
"An international expert panel has established a revised Ghent nosology, which puts more weight on the cardiovascular manifestations and in which aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis are the cardinal clinical features."
The expert-panel criteria themselves, naming aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis as the cardinal features.
PMID:20591885 SUPPORT Other
"In the absence of any family history, the presence of these two manifestations is sufficient for the unequivocal diagnosis of MFS. In absence of either of these two, the presence of a bonafide FBN1 mutation or a combination of systemic manifestations is required."
States how the criteria combine: the two cardinal features suffice without family history, and FBN1 or the systemic features are needed only when one of them is missing.
Genetic Testing for FBN1 Mutations (Positive in affected individuals)
Sequencing of the FBN1 gene to identify pathogenic variants causing Marfan syndrome.
Show evidence (9 references)
PMID:17657824 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Mutations in the FBN1 gene have been characterised in patients affected by Marfan syndrome and Marfan-related disorders."
This study confirms that FBN1 mutations are present in individuals affected by Marfan syndrome.
PMID:26903455 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Genetic testing is aiding rapid diagnosis of Marfan syndrome as a basis for management of eye, heart and skeletal disease."
This article supports the presence of genetic testing for FBN1 mutations in individuals with Marfan syndrome.
PMID:27234404 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Here, we performed genetic testing of the FBN1 gene in 39 Chinese probands with Marfan/Marfan-like syndrome and their related family members by Sanger sequencing."
This research supports the presence of FBN1 mutations in affected individuals through genetic testing.

Ultrasound imaging to measure aortic root diameter and assess cardiac valve function.
Often used to monitor cardiovascular complications.

"The diagnostic reliability as well as the advantage and limitation of these recent diagnostic strategies are discussed; moreover diagnostic concepts for patients with neonatal as well as classic Marfan syndrome are presented and discussed in the context of the clinical management..."
The reference discusses the use of echocardiography, among other diagnostic tools, in the evaluation and management of cardiovascular manifestations in Marfan syndrome patients.
PMID:33843540 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Aortic root dilation is a major complication of Marfan syndrome and is one of the most important criteria in establishing the diagnosis."
The reference highlights that aortic root dilation is a significant complication of Marfan syndrome and is a key criterion for diagnosis, which is typically monitored using echocardiography.
PMID:35550817 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Even in the absence of significant valvular disease, patients with Marfan syndrome (MFS) have evidence of impaired left ventricular (LV) performance, suggestive of a primary cardiomyopathy."
The reference indicates that echocardiography is used to monitor left ventricular performance and other cardiovascular complications in Marfan syndrome patients.

Slit Lamp Exam (Ectopia lentis)
Ophthalmic examination using magnified light to detect lens subluxation and other ocular abnormalities.
Used to detect lens dislocation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24853997 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The position of the lens was noted by observing the eye in different gaze directions in maximal mydriasis during slit lamp examination."
The study confirms that slit lamp examination is used to detect lens dislocation (ectopia lentis) in patients with Marfan syndrome.

Differential Diagnoses

1

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Marfan Syndrome:

Loeys-Dietz Syndrome MONDO:0018954
Overlapping Features Autosomal dominant aortopathy caused by mutations in components of the TGF-beta signaling pathway - most commonly heterozygous TGFBR1, TGFBR2, SMAD3 or TGFB2, with TGFB3, SMAD2 and IPO8 also curated as causative. It shares aortic root aneurysm, overlapping skeletal features (scoliosis, pectus deformity, arachnodactyly, joint laxity) and increased aortic-wall TGF-beta signaling with Marfan syndrome, and both conform to the aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation module, so the two are readily confused when only cardiovascular and skeletal findings are considered.
Distinguishing Features
  • Loeys-Dietz syndrome carries a craniofacial and vascular triad not associated with Marfan syndrome - hypertelorism, bifid uvula or cleft palate, and generalized arterial tortuosity with aneurysm throughout the arterial tree.
  • Ectopia lentis is a cardinal diagnostic feature of Marfan syndrome under the revised Ghent nosology but was absent in every proband of the original Loeys-Dietz cohort (0/16).
  • Loeys-Dietz syndrome follows a more aggressive vascular course, dissecting at younger ages and smaller aortic diameters (mean age at death 26.0 years in the initial cohorts), so surveillance intervals and surgical thresholds differ from those used in Marfan syndrome.
  • The causal gene separates the two entities - FBN1 in Marfan syndrome versus a TGF-beta pathway gene in Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1, TGFBR2, SMAD3, TGFB2, TGFB3, SMAD2, IPO8) - so molecular testing is the definitive discriminator.
Show evidence (6 references)
PMID:16928994 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The disease is characterized by the triad of arterial tortuosity and aneurysms, hypertelorism, and bifid uvula or cleft palate and is caused by heterozygous mutations in the genes encoding transforming growth factor beta receptors 1 and 2"
Establishes the craniofacial and vascular triad plus the TGFBR1/TGFBR2 etiology that separate Loeys-Dietz syndrome from FBN1-related Marfan syndrome.
PMID:16928994 SUPPORT Human clinical
"The natural history of both types was characterized by aggressive arterial aneurysms (mean age at death, 26.0 years)"
Quantifies the more aggressive vascular natural history that makes the distinction from Marfan syndrome clinically consequential.
PMID:15731757 SUPPORT Human clinical
"Ectopia lentis 0/16 (0%) Typical 1/15"
The original cohort's direct comparison table records ectopia lentis in 0 of 16 Loeys-Dietz probands while listing it as typical for Marfan syndrome, supporting ectopia lentis as a discriminating feature.

mechanistic_hypotheses:
- hypothesis_group_id: canonical_fbn1_tgfb_dysregulation_model
  hypothesis_label: Canonical FBN1 Loss / TGF-β Dysregulation Model
  status: CANONICAL
  description: >-
    Heterozygous FBN1 pathogenic variants reduce or qualitatively alter fibrillin-1 microfibrils, the extracellular scaffold that both confers elastic-fiber mechanical integrity and sequesters latent TGF-β complexes. Loss of microfibrillar function therefore produces a dual lesion: (1) structural weakness of elastin-rich tissues (aortic media, suspensory ligament of the lens, dura, lung, skin, mitral valve) and (2) excess bioavailable TGF-β with downstream Smad2/3 and non-canonical pathway activation in aortic wall and other tissues. The result is the characteristic multisystem phenotype — progressive aortic root aneurysm and dissection, ectopia lentis, dolichostenomelia, scoliosis, dural ectasia, mitral valve prolapse, and pulmonary blebs. Losartan and other angiotensin/TGF-β-axis inhibitors that reduce aortic-wall TGF-β signaling provide interventional support for the TGF-β-dysregulation component.
  notes: >-
    Retained as CANONICAL. The 2026 falcon
    hypothesis-search report
    (kb/hypotheses/Marfan_Syndrome/canonical_fbn1_tgfb_dysregulation_model;
    openscientist timed out) confirms the dual-lesion model:
    heterozygous FBN1 variants cause microfibrillar mechanical
    failure AND excess bioavailable TGF-β with downstream Smad2/3
    activation. Three qualifications: (1) the relative contribution
    of structural-weakness vs TGF-β-signaling components varies
    by tissue and variant — ectopia lentis is primarily structural
    while aortic aneurysm involves substantial TGF-β-driven
    medial degeneration and matrix remodeling; (2) losartan's
    clinical benefit on aortic root growth in pediatric MFS is
    modest in head-to-head trials with atenolol, indicating that
    TGF-β antagonism alone does not fully prevent dissection
    risk and that hemodynamic stress reduction remains critical;
    (3) Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1/TGFBR2 mutations) and
    related TGF-β-pathway disorders display a more aggressive
    aneurysm phenotype, indicating that the TGF-β-axis can be
    pathogenic even when fibrillin-1 is intact, qualifying the
    necessity of microfibrillar disruption for the aortic
    phenotype.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39096853
    reference_title: "Generation of Marfan syndrome-specific induced pluripotent stem cells harboring FBN1 mutations."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "Marfan syndrome (MFS) is a hereditary condition caused by mutations in the FBN1 gene."
    explanation: >
      iPSC generation paper from Marfan patient-derived cells with FBN1 mutations;
      used as seed reference for the falcon hypothesis-search deep-research run.
      Documents the canonical FBN1-mutation → fibrillin-1-dysfunction axis at the
      cellular level.

- name: Impaired Mechanotransduction
  description: >-
    Perturbed integrin-fibrillin interactions alter focal adhesion signaling
    and mechanosensing, contributing to maladaptive vascular smooth muscle
    cell responses to hemodynamic stress. Nitric oxide signalling through
    soluble guanylate cyclase and PRKG1 also dysregulates the smooth muscle
    actin cytoskeleton. NO donors produce Marfan-like aortopathy in
    wild-type mice, and inhibiting either enzyme reverses the aortopathy in
    Marfan mice.

- name: Mitochondrial Dysfunction
  description: >-
    Emerging evidence implicates mitochondrial dysfunction and oxidative
    stress in vascular smooth muscle cells and endothelial cells as
    contributors to thoracic aortic aneurysm progression in Marfan syndrome.
    Transcriptomic and metabolic profiling of Marfan mouse and patient
    aortas shows mitochondrial dysfunction with mitochondrial DNA depletion,
    and depleting the mitochondrial transcription factor Tfam in vascular
    smooth muscle cells is enough to cause aortic aneurysm.

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: Analysis of fibrillin protein or gene defects in individuals with related phenotypes has revealed that a perinatal lethal syndrome, termed neonatal Marfan syndrome, is due to FBN1 gene mutations.
    explanation: Direct analysis of fibrillin protein and gene defects in affected individuals supports an abnormal fibrillin-1 protein state across the Marfan spectrum.
  - reference: PMID:22705998
    reference_title: "Marfan syndrome: from gene to therapy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: Although historically Marfan syndrome (MFS) has always been considered as a condition caused by the deficiency of a structural extracellular matrix protein, fibrillin-1, the study of Marfan mouse models and Marfan-related conditions has shifted our current understanding to a pathogenic model that involves dysregulation of the cytokine-transforming growth factor beta (TGF-beta) signaling.
    explanation: Affirms fibrillin-1 deficiency as the underlying protein abnormality, but only partially supports a protein-centric reading of the disease because the review argues the pathogenic model is better framed as TGF-beta dysregulation downstream of that deficiency.
  - reference: PMID:37688493
    reference_title: "The usefulness of the genetic panel in the classification and refinement of diagnostic accuracy of Mexican patients with Marfan syndrome and other connective tissue disorders."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: Marfan syndrome (MFS) is a multisystem genetic disorder with over 3000 mutations described in the fibrillin 1 (FBN1) gene.
    explanation: The very large described FBN1 mutation spectrum supports an abnormal fibrillin-1 protein product as the expected biochemical state in Marfan syndrome.

diagnosis:
- name: Revised Ghent Nosology
  description: >-
    The diagnosis rests on the 2010 revised Ghent nosology, which gives most
    weight to the cardiovascular manifestations. Aortic root aneurysm and
    ectopia lentis are its cardinal clinical features. Without a family
    history, the two together are sufficient for the diagnosis; when either
    is absent, a bona fide FBN1 mutation or a systemic score of the other
    features is required.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: Clinical Evaluation
    term:
      id: NCIT:C124351
      label: Clinical Evaluation
  results: >-
    In the absence of family history, aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis
    together establish the diagnosis. If either is absent, a bona fide FBN1
    mutation or a sufficient systemic score is also required.

    supports: SUPPORT

      nosology, which puts more weight on the cardiovascular manifestations

    explanation: >-
      The expert-panel criteria themselves, naming aortic root aneurysm and

    reference_title: "The revised Ghent nosology for the Marfan syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER

      In the absence of any family history, the presence of these two
      manifestations is sufficient for the unequivocal diagnosis of MFS. In
      absence of either of these two, the presence of a bonafide FBN1
      mutation or a combination of systemic manifestations is required.

    explanation: This study supports the presence of FBN1 mutations in individuals who meet the Ghent criteria for Marfan syndrome.

differential_diagnoses:
- name: Loeys-Dietz Syndrome
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: Loeys-Dietz syndrome
    term:
      id: MONDO:0018954
      label: Loeys-Dietz syndrome
  description: >-
    Autosomal dominant aortopathy caused by mutations in components of the
    TGF-beta signaling pathway - most commonly heterozygous TGFBR1, TGFBR2,
    SMAD3 or TGFB2, with TGFB3, SMAD2 and IPO8 also curated as causative. It
    shares aortic root aneurysm, overlapping skeletal features (scoliosis,
    pectus deformity, arachnodactyly, joint laxity) and increased aortic-wall
    TGF-beta signaling with Marfan syndrome, and both conform to the
    aortopathy_tgfbeta_dysregulation module, so the two are readily confused when
    only cardiovascular and skeletal findings are considered.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Loeys-Dietz syndrome carries a craniofacial and vascular triad not associated with Marfan syndrome - hypertelorism, bifid uvula or cleft palate, and generalized arterial tortuosity with aneurysm throughout the arterial tree.
  - Ectopia lentis is a cardinal diagnostic feature of Marfan syndrome under the revised Ghent nosology but was absent in every proband of the original Loeys-Dietz cohort (0/16).
  - Loeys-Dietz syndrome follows a more aggressive vascular course, dissecting at younger ages and smaller aortic diameters (mean age at death 26.0 years in the initial cohorts), so surveillance intervals and surgical thresholds differ from those used in Marfan syndrome.
  - The causal gene separates the two entities - FBN1 in Marfan syndrome versus a TGF-beta pathway gene in Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1, TGFBR2, SMAD3, TGFB2, TGFB3, SMAD2, IPO8) - so molecular testing is the definitive discriminator.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16928994
    reference_title: "Aneurysm syndromes caused by mutations in the TGF-beta receptor."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The disease is characterized by the triad of arterial tortuosity and aneurysms, hypertelorism, and bifid uvula or cleft palate and is caused by heterozygous mutations in the genes encoding transforming growth factor beta receptors 1 and 2"
    explanation: Establishes the craniofacial and vascular triad plus the TGFBR1/TGFBR2 etiology that separate Loeys-Dietz syndrome from FBN1-related Marfan syndrome.
  - reference: PMID:16928994
    reference_title: "Aneurysm syndromes caused by mutations in the TGF-beta receptor."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The natural history of both types was characterized by aggressive arterial aneurysms (mean age at death, 26.0 years)"
    explanation: Quantifies the more aggressive vascular natural history that makes the distinction from Marfan syndrome clinically consequential.
  - reference: PMID:15731757
    reference_title: "A syndrome of altered cardiovascular, craniofacial, neurocognitive and skeletal development caused by mutations in TGFBR1 or TGFBR2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Ectopia lentis 0/16 (0%) Typical 1/15"
    explanation: The original cohort's direct comparison table records ectopia lentis in 0 of 16 Loeys-Dietz probands while listing it as typical for Marfan syndrome, supporting ectopia lentis as a discriminating feature.
  - reference: PMID:20591885
    reference_title: "The revised Ghent nosology for the Marfan syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "aortic root aneurysm and ectopia lentis are the cardinal clinical features"
    explanation: The revised Ghent nosology makes ectopia lentis a cardinal criterion for Marfan syndrome, which is why its absence redirects the differential toward Loeys-Dietz syndrome.
  - reference: PMID:20591885
    reference_title: "The revised Ghent nosology for the Marfan syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The recognition of variable clinical expression and the recently extended differential diagnosis further confound accurate diagnostic decision making."
    explanation: The nosology panel explicitly identifies the expanded differential diagnosis as a source of diagnostic error, motivating this differential entry.
  - reference: PMID:24577266
    reference_title: "Loeys-Dietz syndrome: a primer for diagnosis and management."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Unlike the increased risk of aortic dissection at or above the 5.0-cm aortic root dimension in Marfan syndrome, dissections have occurred in individuals with LDS 1, 2, or 3 at aortic dimensions of 3.9–4.0 cm"
    explanation: Supplies the aortic-diameter half of the distinguishing feature. Marfan syndrome carries the increased dissection risk at or above a 5.0-cm aortic root, whereas Loeys-Dietz dissections have occurred at 3.9-4.0 cm, which is why surveillance intervals and surgical thresholds differ between the two.
  notes: >-
    Historical nosology note. After a second Marfan-overlapping locus was mapped
    to 3p24.2-p25 and TGFBR2 mutations were identified there, TGF-beta receptor
    aortopathy was labelled "Marfan syndrome type 2" (MFS2, Marfan syndrome type
    II), and reviews as recent as 2020 still present TGFBR1/TGFBR2 disease as a
    Marfan subtype. MONDO has since obsoleted that entity - MONDO:0017308
    "obsolete Marfan syndrome type 2", subset speculative, definition "OBSOLETE.
    Hypothesized form of Marfan; dubious" - with no replacement term. dismech
    therefore curates the TGF-beta receptor aortopathies as Loeys-Dietz syndrome
    (MONDO:0018954), a separate Disease entry reached through this differential,
    and Marfan syndrome carries no type 2 subtype. Note that MONDO retired MFS2
    as a dubious entity rather than asserting that MFS2 is equivalent to
    Loeys-Dietz syndrome; the obsolete CURIE must not be cited as a term
    anywhere in the knowledge base. The two diseases are related as sibling
    members of the Heritable_Thoracic_Aortic_Disease grouping, not as parent and
    subtype.
environmental:
- name: Vigorous Isometric and Competitive Sport Restriction
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Aortic Dissection
    environmental_effect: PROTECTS_AGAINST
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Restriction of vigorous isometric and competitive exertion is intended to prevent aortic
      dissection by limiting acute rises in blood pressure and aortic wall stress.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36072558
      reference_title: "Safety of exercise for adults with thoracic aortic aneurysms and dissections."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Current guidelines for patients with thoracic aortic aneurysms or dissections (TAD) restrict vigorous exertion with the intention to prevent acute aortic dissections."
      explanation: >-
        States that current guidelines restrict vigorous exertion with the intention of preventing
        acute aortic events. An intention, which is what guidelines encode, not a demonstrated
        effect. The exposure binding denotes strenuous exercise rather than its restriction,
        creating a polarity problem for the protective relationship (issue 2656). The intention to
        prevent dissection is not a measured reduction in risk.
    - reference: PMID:9789865
      reference_title: "Exercise and the Marfan syndrome."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "The person with Marfan syndrome is often tall and agile and may unknowingly participate in certain physical activities and sports, putting himself or herself at risk for aortic dissection and sudden death."
      explanation: >-
        Notes that affected people are often tall and agile and may
        unknowingly take part in activities that put them at risk of
        dissection and sudden death. It establishes the hazard the restriction
        addresses.
  description: >
    Avoidance of maximal-effort isometric/static exertion (heavy weight lifting),
    high-intensity exercise, and competitive or contact sport, which acutely raise
    blood pressure and hemodynamic stress on a fragile aortic wall.
  effect: Reduces risk of aortic dissection.
  notes: >
    Deliberately scoped to the restriction half of activity modification. The
    complementary, aortoprotective effect of moderate dynamic (aerobic) activity is
    curated as the separate "Moderate Dynamic Aerobic Activity" exposure below, so
    that exercise-benefit evidence is no longer attached to a restriction claim
    (issue dismech#6953). Both exposures are held simultaneously in current practice:
    exercise intensity and type, not physical activity per se, are what is
    restricted. This node and the moderate-activity node below previously shared
    XCO:0000059, which made the KGX exporter emit two identical subject/predicate/
    object edges; they now carry distinct ECTO terms (ECTO:6000031 strenuous vs
    ECTO:6000002 exercise), which the schema also prefers over XCO, so the split is
    visible downstream. OPEN QUESTION: the exported subject is the exposure
    (strenuous exercise) while this node's effect is phrased for the protective
    restriction, so the edge still reads "strenuous exercise reduces risk". Whether
    to keep both arms PROTECTIVE or re-express this one as "increases risk" is left
    to the structured effect_polarity enum in issue dismech#2656.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:9789865
    reference_title: "Exercise and the Marfan syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: The person with Marfan syndrome is often tall and agile and may unknowingly participate in certain physical activities and sports, putting himself or herself at risk for aortic dissection and sudden death.
    explanation: The article suggests that physical activity restrictions are necessary to prevent aortic dissection in individuals with Marfan syndrome.
  - reference: PMID:36072558
    reference_title: "Safety of exercise for adults with thoracic aortic aneurysms and dissections."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: Current guidelines for patients with thoracic aortic aneurysms or dissections (TAD) restrict vigorous exertion with the intention to prevent acute aortic dissections.
    explanation: Documents that the restriction applies specifically to vigorous exertion and that its stated intent is prevention of acute aortic dissection.
  - reference: PMID:35144638
    reference_title: "Is physical activity a future therapy for patients with Marfan syndrome?"
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: There were potential beneficial effects of PA on arterial structures, but this review also showed deleterious effects when PA was conducted at high intensities, corresponding to 75-85% of the maximal oxygen uptake. However, these effects have only been reported in animal studies.
    explanation: >
      Systematic review supporting an intensity threshold for harm rather than a
      blanket restriction on activity. INDIRECT because the review
      states explicitly that the deleterious high-intensity effects have been
      reported only in animal studies, so it does not establish the harm arm in
      humans. Tagged MODEL_ORGANISM because the specific harm claim this item is
      cited for is animal-derived by the authors' own statement, even though the
      review as a whole also surveys human data.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: Vigorous isometric and competitive sport exposure
    description: Exposure to high-intensity or isometric physical exertion that increases aortic wall stress.
    term:
      id: ECTO:6000031
      label: exposure to strenuous exercise
- name: Moderate Dynamic Aerobic Activity
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Aortic Root Aneurysm
    environmental_effect: PROTECTS_AGAINST
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      The complementary half of activity modification, and the
      better-evidenced of the two protective links: this one has an
      intervention. Patients instructed to walk ten thousand steps a day had a
      significantly lower aortic root Z score than controls, which measures
      the outcome this node holds rather than an intention. Held
      simultaneously with the restriction link by design, since what is
      restricted is exercise intensity and type rather than physical activity
      itself.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36453629
      reference_title: "Can 10 000 Healthy Steps a Day Slow Aortic Root Dilation in Pediatric Patients With Marfan Syndrome?"
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "We instructed patients to take 10 000 steps per day, tracked by an activity tracker... AoR Z score in the intervention group had a significantly lower rate of change per year compared with the controls (rate of change, -0.24 versus +0.008; P=0.01)."
      explanation: >-
        Intervention study in which patients instructed to walk ten thousand
        steps a day had a significantly lower aortic root Z score. A measured
        effect on the dilation this node describes.
    - reference: PMID:28947563
      reference_title: "Cardiovascular Benefits of Moderate Exercise Training in Marfan Syndrome: Insights From an Animal Model."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: "Over the 5-month experimental period, aortic root dilation rate was significantly greater in the sedentary MF group, compared with the wild-type group (∆mm, 0.27±0.07 versus 0.13±0.02, respectively). Exercise significantly blunted the aortic root dilation rate in MF mice compared with sedentary MF littermates (∆mm, 0.10±0.04 versus 0.27±0.07, respectively)."
      explanation: >-
        Aortic root dilation was significantly greater in sedentary mutant
        animals than in wild type over five months. Converges from an animal
        model, so it is carried at partial and not alone.
  description: >
    Regular moderate-intensity dynamic (aerobic) activity undertaken within the
    restrictions above - for example a 10,000-steps-per-day walking target - rather
    than sedentary avoidance of all exertion.
  effect: Reduces risk of progressive aortic root dilation.
  notes: >
    Split out from the former single "Physical Activity Restrictions" node so that
    exercise-benefit evidence attaches to a claim it actually supports (issue dismech#6953).
    The human trial evidence is pediatric and single-centre and the supporting
    aortic-wall data are murine, so the aortoprotective claim should be read as
    emerging rather than settled.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36453629
    reference_title: "Can 10 000 Healthy Steps a Day Slow Aortic Root Dilation in Pediatric Patients With Marfan Syndrome?"
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: We instructed patients to take 10 000 steps per day, tracked by an activity tracker... AoR Z score in the intervention group had a significantly lower rate of change per year compared with the controls (rate of change, -0.24 versus +0.008; P=0.01).
    explanation: A prescribed moderate dynamic activity target slowed the rate of aortic root growth relative to controls, directly supporting an aortoprotective effect of moderate aerobic activity.
  - reference: PMID:36072558
    reference_title: "Safety of exercise for adults with thoracic aortic aneurysms and dissections."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: Moderate intensity exercise is safe and feasible for many TAD patients.
    explanation: >
      Establishes that moderate-intensity exercise is safe and feasible in thoracic
      aortic disease - a necessary but not sufficient part of this node's claim.
      PARTIAL rather than SUPPORT because safety is not aortoprotection: the paper
      does not report that moderate activity slows aortic root dilation. Note also
      that the cohort is mixed heritable-plus-degenerative TAD (31 cases, only 2 with
      Marfan syndrome), so it generalizes to Marfan rather than measuring it directly.
      The aortoprotective claim itself rests on PMID:36453629 and PMID:28947563.
  - reference: PMID:28947563
    reference_title: "Cardiovascular Benefits of Moderate Exercise Training in Marfan Syndrome: Insights From an Animal Model."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: Over the 5-month experimental period, aortic root dilation rate was significantly greater in the sedentary MF group, compared with the wild-type group (∆mm, 0.27±0.07 versus 0.13±0.02, respectively). Exercise significantly blunted the aortic root dilation rate in MF mice compared with sedentary MF littermates (∆mm, 0.10±0.04 versus 0.27±0.07, respectively).
    explanation: Moderate exercise training blunted aortic root dilation in a Marfan mouse model, giving the aortoprotective direction of effect a mechanistic counterpart in vivo.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: Moderate dynamic aerobic activity exposure
    description: Exposure to moderate-intensity dynamic (aerobic) physical activity.
    term:
      id: ECTO:6000002
      label: exposure to exercise

    cardiovascular complications

Gap review: five of six pathophysiology nodes carried no evidence. Added primary evidence to each: - Dysregulated TGF-beta Signaling: SUPPORT PMID:12598898 (lung, fibrillin-1-deficient mice), PMID:16601194 (losartan and TGF-beta neutralisation prevent aneurysm); REFUTE PMID:28119285 (young Fbn1 C1039G/+ mice develop aortopathy with no detectable SMC TGF-beta change, worsened by SMC TBRII loss). Noted, not changed: the genetic section lists TGFBR1, TGFBR2, SMAD2, SMAD3 and ACTA2, which cause other heritable aortopathies (Loeys-Dietz, familial TAAD) rather than Marfan syndrome; worth a separate review. Claude Managed ▸
1. Disease Information
claude-opus-5-5 229 citations 2026-11-10T16:27:50.563740

1. Disease Information

Overview. OMIM describes MFS as "A heritable disorder of fibrous connective tissue, Marfan syndrome (MFS) shows striking pleiotropism and clinical variability." (OMIM:154700), with "The cardinal features occur in 3 systems--skeletal, ocular, and cardiovascular" (OMIM:154700). Orphanet defines it as "a systemic disease of connective tissue characterized by a variable combination of cardiovascular, musculo-skeletal, ophthalmic and pulmonary manifestations" (ORPHA:558). A Nature Reviews Disease Primer states that MFS "is an autosomal dominant, age-related but highly penetrant condition with substantial intrafamilial and interfamilial variability" (PMID:34475413), and that "The most prominent manifestations of MFS are asymptomatic aortic root aneurysms, aortic dissections, dislocation of the ocular lens (ectopia lentis) and skeletal abnormalities that are characterized by overgrowth of the long bones" (PMID:34475413). GeneReviews describes "a broad phenotypic continuum ranging from mild (features of Marfan syndrome in one or a few systems) to severe and rapidly progressive neonatal multiorgan disease" (PMID:20301510).

Key identifiers. MONDO:0007947 (Marfan syndrome) (MONDO:0007947); OMIM:154700 (OMIM:154700); Orphanet ORPHA:558 "Marfan syndrome" and ORPHA:284963 "Marfan syndrome type 1" (clinical subtype exactly mapped to OMIM 154700) (ORPHA:558); ICD-10 Q87.4 and ICD-11 LD28.01 (both exact mappings in Orphanet) (ORPHA:558); MeSH D008382 "Marfan Syndrome" (MeSH:D008382); UMLS C0024796 and MedDRA 10026829 (ORPHA:558); NCIT:C34807 (Marfan Syndrome) (see ols-chebi-ncit-terms.md). The causal gene is FBN1, HGNC:3603 (fibrillin 1), OMIM gene entry 134797, Entrez Gene 2200, Ensembl ENSG00000166147, MANE transcript NM_000138.5, UniProt P35555 (HGNC:3603). Related MONDO terms: MONDO:0017309 (neonatal Marfan syndrome), MONDO:0017310 (Marfan and Marfan-related disorder), MONDO:0018954 (Loeys-Dietz syndrome) (see ols-mondo-hgnc-terms.md).

Synonyms. MONDO lists the exact synonyms "MFS, MFS1, Marfan syndrome type 1, Marfan syndrome, Marfan syndrome, type 1, Marfan's syndrome" (MONDO:0007947); MedlinePlus lists "Marfan's syndrome" and "MFS" (https://medlineplus.gov/genetics/condition/marfan-syndrome/). The term "Marfan syndrome type 2" is obsolete: GeneReviews notes "This term is no longer in use" and that the phenotype "is now known to be Loeys-Dietz syndrome" (PMID:20301510). GeneReviews also recommends abandoning "neonatal Marfan syndrome" as a discrete category in favour of "early onset" and "rapidly progressive" (PMID:20301510), whereas MONDO retains MONDO:0017309 (neonatal Marfan syndrome).

Source of information. The descriptive information in this report comes overwhelmingly from aggregated disease-level resources (OMIM, Orphanet, MONDO, GeneReviews, HPO annotations) and from published cohort studies, registries and trials, not from individual-patient EHR extraction. Several epidemiological studies did use national administrative registries built from individual records (Danish, Taiwanese and Korean health systems) (PMID:26631233; PMID:24388020; PMID:28244281).

2. Etiology

Disease causal factors. MFS is genetic. "MFS is caused by pathogenetic variants in FBN1, which encodes fibrillin-1, a major structural component of the extracellular matrix that provides support to connective tissues, particularly in arteries, the pericondrium and structures in the eye" (PMID:34475413). The link was established when "the fibrillin gene is linked to the Marfan phenotype (theta = 0.00; logarithm of the odds (lod) = 3.9)" and a de novo missense mutation was found in sporadic cases (PMID:1852208). No environmental, infectious or toxic primary cause exists.

Genetic risk factors. The causal factor is a heterozygous FBN1 pathogenic variant; "Up to 25% of individuals with MFS have de novo variants" (PMID:34475413). Variant class modifies risk: truncating/premature termination codon (haploinsufficient, HI) variants carry higher aortic and survival risk than dominant-negative (DN) missense variants (PMID:26787436; PMID:33731877; PMID:41309212), and cysteine-loss in-frame variants are more severe than other in-frame variants (PMID:33731877). Genome-wide modifier searches in 1,070 FBN1 carriers found "A polygenic model involving at least nine modifier loci (named gMod-M1-9)" (PMID:30087447) and a second pathogenic event in another thoracic aortic aneurysm gene or COL4A1 in nine individuals (PMID:30087447). A family history of severe cardiovascular disease is an independent risk marker: "aortic dilation is greater and life expectancy shorter in those with a family history of severe cardiovascular manifestations" (PMID:7560600).

Non-genetic risk factors (modifiers of expression, not causes). Male sex is associated with earlier and more frequent aortic events: in the Danish nationwide cohort, "Male patients had a significant higher risk of an aortic event at a younger age with a hazard ratio of 1.75" (PMID:27550511), and in GenTAC, adult males more often had root dilatation (92% vs 84%) and prophylactic root replacement (47% vs 24%) (PMID:28600386). In a Mayo Clinic cohort, "Among 783 patients (443 men, 340 women), men more often had ascending aortic aneurysm (89.1% vs 71.1%), any aneurysm (91.0% vs 75.3%), and any extra-aortic aneurysm (36.8% vs 25.9%) (all P ≤ 0.001)." while "Dissection prevalence was similar by sex." (PMID:42475821); a German single-centre series found that "Male patients had a significantly higher probability of experiencing an aortic event (P = 0.015) compared to female patients." (PMID:35543473), and across GenTAC conditions "Thoracic aortic dissections occurred with equal gender frequency" while women "had fewer total aortic surgeries" (PMID:23444191). Smoking is mentioned by the 2022 ACC/AHA guideline: "Most individuals with aortic disease distal to the root have had previous root replacement or smoke cigarettes." (PMID:36322642). Pregnancy increases dissection risk (rate ratio 8.4 vs non-pregnant periods) (PMID:27515814). Late diagnosis is itself a risk factor: "Multivariate Cox regression analysis confirmed late diagnosis as predictor of delayed RAA (hazard ratio, 8.01" (PMID:39306029). Hypertension prevalence was 28.8% among young MFS patients with type B dissection in GenTAC, and the authors concluded that control of hypertension is "an essential component of care to" decrease the risk of type B dissection (PMID:31376376). Age is a determinant of penetrance of several features (see Sections 3 and 8).

Protective factors. No protective genetic variant has been identified in the retrieved evidence. In mice, higher residual wild-type Fbn1 expression is protective: Lima et al. report "a strong negative correlation between overall levels of Fbn1 expression and the severity of the phenotypes, corroborating the suggested protective role of normal fibrillin-1" (PMID:21152435), and in C1039G/+ mice "introduction of a WT FBN1 transgene on a heterozygous C1039G background rescues aortic phenotype" (PMID:15254584). A sex-hormone mechanism has been proposed from human, mouse and in vitro data: "the female hormone 17β-estradiol stimulates aortic smooth muscle cells to produce fibrillin-1 in vitro" (PMID:28708846). Moderate aerobic exercise is protective in mice — "moderate, but not strenuous, exercise protects aortic structure and function in a mouse model of Marfan syndrome" (PMID:28385916), and in Fbn1 C1039G/+ mice trained on a treadmill "aortic root diameter was smaller than in their Sed littermates" (PMID:28947563) — but no human outcome trial exists. Protective environmental/therapeutic factors are β-blockers and ARBs (Section 12) and, observationally, ARB use was associated with fewer type B dissections (hazard ratio 0.3) (PMID:25614422). Early diagnosis is protective in the sense that "diagnosing MFS is lifesaving and improves mortality risk by reducing the risk of aorta dissection" (PMID:27550511).

Gene–environment interactions. The best-documented gene–drug interaction is with calcium channel blockers: "Marfan mice treated with CCBs show accelerated aneurysm expansion, rupture, and premature lethality" via a PKCβ/ERK1/2- and AT1R-dependent pathway, and "patients with Marfan syndrome and other forms of inherited thoracic aortic aneurysm taking CCBs display increased risk of aortic dissection and need for aortic surgery" (PMID:26506064). Fluoroquinolones accelerate aortic enlargement in Fbn1C1041G/+ mice, with dissection 25% vs 47% and rupture 5% vs 25% (vehicle vs ciprofloxacin) (PMID:34586071), but a Taiwanese case-crossover study of 550 patients (415 with MFS) found "Fluoroquinolone use was not associated with an increased risk of AA/AD in patients with congenital aortic disease, including Marfan syndrome" (PMID:36823078). Pregnancy is a physiological stressor that interacts with genotype (PMID:27515814). Genetic background modifies disease in mice: onset "is earlier in the 129/Sv than in C57BL/6 background, indicating the existence of genetic modifiers of MFS between these two mouse strains" (PMID:21152435). No entries for MFS in CTD or GxE databases were retrieved.

3. Phenotypes

The table lists principal phenotypes with HPO terms (all IDs and labels from ols-hpo-terms-marfan.md or hpo-annotations-omim-154700.md). Frequencies attributed to HPO annotations are the counts attached to OMIM:154700 in the HPO annotation file, which derive mostly from single cohorts and should be read as indicative (OMIM:154700).
